# Slay. Idle. Repeat. — Implementation Tracker

This is the single tracking document for turning the design set in [`game-design/`](game-design/00_README_INDEX.md) into a shipped game. It follows the build order authored in `16_DECISION_LOG.md` Part D and the architecture fixed by docs `14`, `23` and `30`.

## How this document is used

- **One interactive kickoff phase per milestone.** Before a milestone starts, the open questions in its *Kickoff decisions* block are resolved with the product owner, and the task list is adjusted. After kickoff, agents implement the tasks autonomously (via the `feature-oneshot` pipeline) and update the status column here.
- **Statuses:** `⬜ todo` · `🔄 in progress` · `🔍 in review` · `✅ done` · `⛔ blocked (note why)`
- **Spec references** (`14 §8.1` etc.) point into `game-design/`. Where docs disagree, the authority chain ruled in `16` A7 applies — implement against the referenced authority doc, not a summarising one.
- Tasks are sized to be one autonomous feature-pipeline run each (spec → tests → implementation → reviews). If a task turns out too large, split it here first, then implement.
- Milestones are ordered, but the **workstreams marked ∥ can run in parallel** with the mainline (noted per milestone).
- **After each milestone, `milestone-review` runs a whole-milestone quality pass and a retro.** Its output lands in `.claude/retros/M<N>.md`, and the distilled rules in **`.claude/retros/STEERING.md`** — which `kickoff-milestone` reads and pastes into every dispatched agent prompt. Steering rules bind later milestones; read them before planning one.

Read this block before planning M6, M8 or M9: four rulings change a design doc, and two change a contract every M6 calibration target is derived against.

## M0 — Foundations, CI & week-1 spikes

**Goal:** the solution skeleton, the deterministic primitives, and the two schedule-risk spikes — before any feature work.
**Exit:** CI builds everything, boots the compose stack, runs the architecture tests; both spikes have written findings.
✅ **Complete.** `feature-M0-*` → `milestone/M0` → `review/M0` → **`main`, merged 2026-08-11.** Retro: `.claude/retros/M0.md`.

**Kickoff decisions** — ✅ resolved 2026-08-11 (record: `.claude/.milestone-runs/M0/kickoff.md`)
1. **O18 — bundle/package identifier: `de.ludwigso.slayidlerepeat`.** Reverse-DNS of a controlled domain. Subscription product ID unchanged (`slayidlerepeat.plus.monthly`). → fold into `00` §0a and `16` Part B as **O18 closed**.
2. **Repo/branching/versioning:** base branch renamed `master` → `main`. Branches: `milestone/M<N>` ← `feature-M<N>-<nn>-<slug>`, review on `review/M<N>`. **Three independent version numbers**, each hand-bumped with a written rule and a CI pin: assembly SemVer `0.x.y` in one `Directory.Build.props` (→ `1.0.0` at soft launch), wire `PROTOCOL_VERSION` (int, from 1, `14` §16.1), snapshot `SchemaVersion` (int, from 1, `14` §16.6).
3. **Spikes signed off to run first, in parallel with scaffolding** — dispatched in wave 1. Scope bounded by the available hardware: **O23's Android leg is executed for real locally**; the **iOS leg is a written CI recipe + findings** (no Mac / Apple Developer account), split out as M0-05b. **O14 is a source-and-docs verdict** on the MAX Godot plugin (no AppLovin account needed to read an MIT repo).
4. **Runtime pin: `net8.0` for every project**, so `Core` stays loadable by the Godot client. Godot version pinned by the O23 spike to the newest 4.x stable that passes the full custom-export path.
5. **CI: GitHub Actions**, authored now under `.github/workflows/`. ⚠️ **No git remote exists** — nothing can actually run until the user creates the repo and pushes, so M0-02's deliverable is *authored and locally validated*, not *observed green*.
6. **Local observability (M0-03):** OTel Collector + Jaeger + Prometheus + Grafana in compose. Sentry/PostHog adapters wired but pointed at a local sink — no cloud account, no multi-GB self-hosted stacks.

**Exit criteria — verified on `review/M0`, 2026-08-11** (`.claude/retros/M0.md`)

| Criterion | Verdict | How it is known |

| CI builds everything | ✅ **met with caveat** | Clean Release build, 34 projects, 0 warnings (warnings-as-errors on). ⚠️ **No workflow has ever run on a runner — there is no git remote.** Every job's real work lives in `build/ci/*.ps1` and was executed locally; the GitHub Actions wiring itself is unverified. |
| Boots the compose stack | ✅ met | `config` → `up -d --wait` (8 services healthy, 2 init containers clean) → `/health` 200 → integration 3/3 → `down -v`, run twice on the integration branch and again after the review fixes. |

**Carried forward from the M0 review** (recorded, not fixed here)
- 🔴 **M1 will hit a wall at M1-11.** `30` §6 shows `ContentSnapshot.LoadFromDisk(…)` on the Core type, but `14` §6 says loading is I/O and belongs in an adapter — M0-09 implemented the latter. `30` §6 *also* requires `InMemoryGame` to depend on `Core` alone, mechanically enforced by `The_whole_game_is_playable_from_Core_alone`. So `InMemoryGame` cannot build a `ContentSnapshot`, and the tempting escape hatch puts filesystem I/O back inside `Core`. **→ M1 kickoff decision.** Recommended: the harness takes a pre-built `ContentSnapshot`, and `EconomySim` becomes a small composition root over `{Core, Application, Adapters.Content.LocalFile}`.
- 🔴 **`Position` write-back has no choke point.** It is authoritative run state, but nothing yet persists it. A handler that draws and forgets to persist its counter breaks determinism *silently and unreproducibly*. **→ M1 kickoff.**
- **M1-12 is already done.** It asks for "the ten Core architecture tests"; all ten exist and are live from M0-08. Restate it as *verify the vacuous rules woke up, and delete the now-false "vacuous until M1" comments*.

## M1 — Core domain skeleton & `InMemoryGame`

**Goal:** `GameRules.Apply` exists, the aggregates exist, and a full (rules-light) game session runs in memory from `Core` alone.
**Exit:** `InMemoryGame` drives a multi-day player through commands with the day cycle, energy and currencies working; all ten Core architecture tests green.

**Kickoff decisions** — ✅ resolved 2026-08-11 (record: `.claude/.milestone-runs/M1/kickoff.md`)

1. **The command vocabulary is frozen at 49 commands — 19 run + 30 meta — as the `14` §2.3 *table* stands.** 🔴 The table has always had 30 meta rows; its own header ("Meta commands (29)") and this tracker's "48 commands" were both miscounts. Errata, not a scope change. M1-02 declares all 49 types and registers every one in the dispatch table. Additions afterwards are logged decisions in `16`. ⚠️ **Guild actions have no commands in the registry at all** — an exhaustiveness hole `14` §2.3 must close at the **M14 kickoff** if guilds ship.
2. **Snapshot `SchemaVersion`: one global integer** (as M0 shipped it), **no migration code before soft launch.** `Rehydrate` hard-fails loudly on an unknown version; pre-launch dev data is disposable. Written migrations become mandatory from the first production build (**M18**). Every bump still adds a new field-order pin entry and never edits an old one.
3. **v1 surface scope confirmed** (steering S15): **PvP/Ghost Duel (M12), Resource Dungeons (M10), Live-ops events (M13) and Guilds (M14) are all in v1.** M14 remains the designated schedule-relief valve. Only PvP touches M1 — its 3 commands stay in the frozen vocabulary.
4. **`ContentSnapshot` × `InMemoryGame` (M0-review 🔴 resolved):** the harness takes a **pre-built** `ContentSnapshot` — `InMemoryGame(ContentSnapshot, ulong seed, VirtualClock)`. It never loads. `ContentSnapshot`'s existing public constructor over in-memory documents lets `Core.Tests` build hermetic snapshots with no I/O. Anything wanting the real `game-data` is a composition root over `{Core, Application, Adapters.Content.LocalFile}` — `EconomySim` (M6), the balance harness, and one real-data smoke test in `Application.Tests`. **`30` §6's `ContentSnapshot.LoadFromDisk(…)` example is a documented erratum.**
5. **RNG `Position` write-back (M0-review 🔴 resolved): `Apply` owns it.** Handlers never construct a `DeterministicRng`; `Apply` hands each run handler a `RunRngScope` built from `runSeed` + the committed positions, folds the scope's final positions into the new `Run` itself, and rejects a handler that hand-wrote a position. Enforced by a new architecture rule making `DeterministicRng`'s constructor unreachable outside `Core/Rng` and the scope, with a red-then-green demonstration (S1). Built in **M1-06** even though no M1 handler draws, so M3 inherits it instead of inventing it under pressure.
6. **FluentAssertions is removed from the repository entirely; Shouldly replaces it.** 561 call sites across 28 files. Lands as **M1-00, alone in wave 1**, so every later agent writes Shouldly from its first line.

**Assumptions recorded after the interactive window** (full reasoning in the kickoff record)
- **Energy accrual never loses a fraction to call frequency.** Whole units only; the accrual anchor advances by `wholeUnits × 4 min`, never to `NowUtc`. Property test: N small `AdvanceTime` steps ≡ one big step.
- **The game week starts Monday 05:00 UTC** — derived from `27` §4's guild-boss cadence, consistent with the 05:00 UTC game day. No doc states it generally.
- **`GOLD` is run-scoped** (`tuning/currencies.json`), so the "8 currencies" of M1-04 are 7 on `Player` + `GOLD` on `Run`. Both aggregates' currency fields are subject to `Every_currency_mutation_emits_CurrencyChanged`.
- **`WorldSlice` is declared `(Player, Run?)` in M1.** `GuildView`/`GhostSnapshot` arrive with M14/M12 — `WorldSlice` is not persisted, so a later nullable field costs no `SchemaVersion` bump, and an empty placeholder type is the plausible-looking hole S6 forbids. Deliberate divergence from `30` §4.1's four-field sketch.
- **One M1 gap register**, not one per task (S4): a single self-expiring list covering the not-yet-implemented commands, the `AdvanceTime` boundaries whose state does not exist yet, and M1-09's deferred draws. Each entry names its owning milestone **and** a type or member that must not yet exist; the build fails as a stale exemption the moment that member appears.
- **`SchemaVersion` is the first field of every `*Snapshot`** (`30` §11.3) and `CanonicalStateWriter` refuses any public property outside a record's primary constructor. Both live from M0-07; M1-04/M1-05 must add their records to `SnapshotFieldOrder.json` under SchemaVersion 1 in the same change (the pin file's README authorises exactly this first population).

**Exit criteria — verified on `review/M1`, 2026-08-13**

| Criterion | Verdict | How it is known |
|---|---|---|
| `InMemoryGame` drives a multi-day player through commands with the day cycle, energy and currencies working | ✅ **met with a scope caveat** | Driven, not asserted, by 10 tests in `InMemoryGameDayCycleTests`: 8h/12h accrual with the overflow cascade, A1's remainder across 480 one-minute commands, **168 commands over 14 days paying exactly 14 refills**, the Monday-05:00 boundary from a Wednesday start, a 100-day absence landing on one boundary step, A6's zero-delta row in both arms. 180-day player in **15.4 ms** against a 200 ms budget. ⚠️ **"currencies" is exercised only for `ENERGY`** — no M1 command moves another — the other five are covered at the aggregate seam in `PlayerWalletTests`/`RunWalletTests`. ⚠️ **"commands" is carrying one command**: `BEGIN_SESSION` is the only `Handled` row |
| All ten Core architecture tests green | ✅ **met** | M1-12 verified `30` §9's ten **arm by arm**, each mutated separately with literal output: **8 fully live · 2 partially live · 1 vacuous-by-design sub-clause**. The two partials are subject-less **by intent** — the `IClockPort` and `Application.Ports` arms have no subject because those names must never appear in `Core`. The review's independent count over the whole 67-rule suite: **62 fully live**, and the two it found vacuous for a *bad* reason (the guild/combat isolation rules) now carry `Pending` rows naming M2 and M14 |

**Overarching tasks advanced:** **X-02** 38 → **67** rules. **X-01** unchanged (4-dp rounding is M2; cross-platform `LogHash` CI is M5-12). **X-03/X-04** unchanged. **X-05/X-06/X-08** not started.

**Carried forward from waves 1–2 — each has a named owner, none is fixed yet** (steering S16)

| # | Finding | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 🔴 **A public *field* on a snapshot record hashes as zero bytes.** `CanonicalStateWriter`'s `CanonicalProperties` counts *properties*, so the converse check it applies to properties has no field equivalent. Pre-existing in M0-07 and harmless until the first snapshot exists — which is the next task to touch it. Fix it there, with a red-then-green proof | **M1-04** |
| 2 | **`Core_internal_layering_holds` does not catch a `const` reference.** The compiler inlines a `const` as a literal, so no type reference reaches the IL: a `Primitives` type can read a `Content` const with the rule silent. Found by M1-01's first S1 mutation, which passed when it should not have | **M1-12** |
| 3 | **Pre-agreed narrow exemption for the entitlement IL backstop, if needed.** `No_entitlement_branch_outside_a_composition_root` fires on compiler-generated record plumbing, making value equality on any entitlement-bearing record impossible by *any* means. If `PlayerSnapshot` carries entitlement state (`30` §4 lists it under `Player`; `30` §3 and `12` §2.1 put it on the session), skip methods that are **both** `[CompilerGenerated]` **and** named record plumbing — leaving author-written `Equals` and every lambda body caught. Not applied now: relaxing a live shared rule so new code fits is a decision, not a side effect, and nothing needs it yet | **M1-04** (conditional) |
| 4 | **Commands should declare their own wire name.** M1-07 needed a type-name→wire-name mapping for the `CommandSeed` pin and had to build a documented *heuristic*, because no authored scheme exists. Having the command type declare it removes the guess | **M1-02** |
| 5 | **Move `CurrencyChanged` from `Pending` to `Live`** when it lands, beside the `CurrencyId` row already there. `Every_currency_mutation_emits_CurrencyChanged` stays vacuous until M1-04 brings the first currency field, so a rename in the meantime would silence it permanently | **M1-03** |
| 6 | **A meta-rule reconciling `Domain.*Type` constants against the registers.** `Domain.GameContextType` was declared, referenced by nothing, and in neither register — deleted at wave 2, but only because a human read the file. The same class as S4's known limit, one level up | **M1-12** |
| 7 | **`14` contradicts itself on what the kill switches gate.** §10's remote-config row says "PvP, individual ad placements, **content versions** and the Plus offer"; §14 says "PvP, each ad placement, the Plus offer, and **each chapter**". M1-07 implemented chapters, because §14 is the kill-switch sentence. Separately, `23` §4.2 declares `IRemoteConfigPort` with an open string-keyed surface plus a singular `FeatureFlag` type that M1-07's deliberately-closed record has no place for | **M5-10** |

Two materially different states, one hash, every suite green; it would have broken client-mirror verification, the parity test and the reconnect chaos test. **The test review was wrong about why it matters and the test caught the reviewer:** Roslyn's synthesized record `Equals` compares every *instance field*, so equality does see it — the divergence is real, not "equality is blind too". **`Every_currency_mutation_emits_CurrencyChanged` is now awake** over real production fields; waking it cost one narrow clause, because a positional record compiles each component to a compiler-generated `init` setter, so `PlayerSnapshot.set_Wallet` writes a currency field, is not a constructor and is not named `Rehydrate` — the exemption is now `[CompilerGenerated] && init-only`, with a hand-written-init fixture proving the first conjunct carries weight. 🔒 **Asking "will this serve the next consumer?" found five defects, three before a line was written** — M1-06 would not have *compiled* (namespace `Model.Player` + type `Player` shadows the type for all of `Model/`, CS0118, and `30` §4.1's `WorldSlice` lives there); a public enum under `Model/` fails `Apply_is_the_only_public_mutation` on its `value__` field; M1-05 could not have written `START_RUN` without `02` §2's lifetime `runCounter`, and adding it later costs a `SchemaVersion` bump that `Rehydrate` turns into a hard refusal of every stored row; M1-08 would have wiped the counters it called to reset; and M1-08 could have re-granted energy forever. Architecture 52 → 54 |
| M1-05 | `Run` aggregate as child of `Player` — rescoped at wave 5 to what has element types: ownership, `runSeed`, the per-stream draw counters, position index, HP, run `GOLD`, per-run ad uses, `RunSnapshot`. Board, drafted perks, held consumables, pending fork, curses and the run phase are `GapRegister` entries against M3 | 30 §4 | ✅ **verified in the M1 review** · · `feature-M1-05-run-aggregate` — 🔒 **the stream counters are the deliverable.** A sparse map keyed by registered stream name, because `14` §8.1's ninth row (`minigame:{index}`) is parameterised and nine fields cannot hold `minigame:7` at all. Four properties make M1-06's single-seam write-back enforceable: `CommitStreamPositions` is the **only** writer; it takes the *whole* map and **refuses a dropped key**, so a handler that hand-wrote one position would have to reconstruct the entire committed set — a superset contract, not a merge; a value below the committed one **throws** rather than returning a `RejectionReason`, because a rejection hands the corrupt scope back to the player as a polite "no" and leaves the run in it; and the map is replaced wholesale with an `Ordinal` copy, since any other comparer round-trips to a different hash than it was stored under. **`combat` does not mean what the other positions mean** — it is re-rooted at `battleSeed`, so the `runSeed`-rooted stream is consumed exactly once per battle and its position is the next `battleIndex`; draws *inside* a battle restart at 0 and are never persisted, which is what makes `14` §8.1's "a revived battle restarts from draw 0" true by construction. 🔴 **The currency rule was blind to run Gold.** `CurrencyFields()` recognises a field by *type* or by *name*, and run Gold is a bare `long` — a `long _gold` matches neither, so the rule would not have seen the game's only run-scoped currency. Named `_wallet`, with a floor row pinning `Run::_wallet` so a rename fails the build. Also **closes the exposed-collection hole M1-04 documented but left open**: `public IReadOnlyList<int> P => _array;` on a `Model/` type passed 54/54 before the fix. Architecture 54 → 55 |

A hand-write is detected by diffing the working run's positions — captured **after** `AdvanceTime`, before the handler — against what it carries afterwards. `combat` is special-cased **by refusal**: `Stream(Combat)` throws and `BeginBattle()` is the only door. 🔴 **Code review found the hole in the first design:** `FoldRngPositions` returned early on a null scope, but a **meta** command *is* dispatched with a run in the slice, so a `CommandKind.Meta` handler could have hand-written the run's `14` §8.1 counters undetected. **The dispatch row carries the wire name**, beside the handler and the kind — the one place a `Type` is resolvable without an instance — which **deletes M1-07's type-name heuristic** and its `OpenPvPCommand → OPEN_PV_P` known limit outright (**carry-forward 4 closed**). The mechanism was verified to work for M1-02 by reading `Il.OperandTypes`: its `GenericInstanceMethod` arm sits above the `MethodReference` arm, so `Deferred<RollDiceCommand>` does register the type — without it all 49 would have read as unhandled the day M1-02 landed. **Carry-forward 9 closed:** `PrintMembers` on `DomainEvent` + `CurrencyChanged` + a rule checking `[CompilerGenerated]` rather than mere presence (every record has one). The behavioural test uses **`sv-SE`, not `de-DE`** — German renders a negative integer with an ordinary hyphen, so a de-DE test would have proved nothing. Architecture 55 → 58 |

Two guards the review found could not have failed — a `with` expression bypassed all three null guards (property initializers run only in the primary constructor), and the no-static-factory rule used `GetMethods`, so a static clock-reading factory *property* was invisible to the rule written to forbid exactly it. `Core_internal_layering_holds` gains a row: `Primitives`/`Content`/`Rng` never reach **up** into the Core root |

Pinned by a 2,000-case property test. **That test was itself defective on first attempt** (21 failures instead of 1,487): spans drawn uniformly over 40 days are 7,200+ units against banks holding 400, so 99 % saturated and identical under both implementations — fixing the *sampling* is what gave it teeth. Same shape in the Reserve-never-regenerates test, where the one case **named** for the property was the one case that could not fail. 🔴 **`EnergyBanks` moved to `Core/Primitives/` as a public positional record struct** (`EnergyTuning` → `Content/`): `Model` may not reference `Rules`, so `Player` could never have named it under `Rules/` — which made `30` §11.5's own invariant *"Energy never exceeds max + reserve"* unimplementable as written, and the internal shape failed `CanonicalStateWriter` so it could not have travelled in a snapshot either. **One accessibility keyword away from being M1-04's problem.** `MaxEnergy` corrected to `baseMax + perLevel × (level − 1)` — four numbers in `10` §3 require a starting player to have exactly 120. `InvalidTunableException` completes the `ContentException` family's missing fourth case |
| M1-11 | `InMemoryGame(ContentSnapshot, seed, VirtualClock)` + `VirtualClock`; perf target: 180-day player < 200 ms | 30 §6 | ✅ **verified in the M1 review** · · `feature-M1-11-in-memory-game` — **180-day player in 15.4 ms against the 200 ms budget (13× headroom)**; a gap of 1, 30, 180 or 3650 days all cost ~24 µs, so a ten-year absence costs what a one-day absence costs. `EnergyTuning.Read` is **27.7 %** of the drive. 🔒 **It corrected the premise of its own brief:** the rule has **three arms** — the closure BFS has asserted since M0 and never depended on `InMemoryGame`; only the public-harness arm had never run; and a **presence arm did not exist**, so the rule reported success over the *absence* of the type its own name is about. Architecture 63 → 67 |

---

## M2 — Effect DSL & combat simulation

**Goal:** the one interpreter every game effect compiles into, and the deterministic auto-battle core — "the most important document for the implementer" (05).
**Exit:** all **44** ops / 23 triggers / 23 conditions unit-tested; a full fight simulates in < 5 ms; all 8 bosses run from DSL data with zero bespoke code; DSL parity test (10 000 random builds) green.
*(⚠️ **43 → 44**: `RANDOM_OUTCOME` was added under **R20** by `18` §10's extension procedure, because `17` §9's Dicelord *Roll of Fate* — a d6 with three mutually exclusive weighted outcomes — had no construct at all, and `18` §4's conditions are pure functions of state while a draw is not. The alternative was the per-boss branching `17` §11 claims does not exist. `18` §11's pinned count now reads `44 ops = 41 + CLEAR_SUMMONS + STAT_COPY + RANDOM_OUTCOME`.)*

**Kickoff decisions** — ✅ resolved 2026-08-12 (record: `.claude/.milestone-runs/M2/kickoff.md`)

1. **The four `17` combat rulings are confirmed exactly as written.** (a) `targetPriority`: an int on enemy definitions, default `0`; the hero targets the **highest**, ties by **lowest current HP**; sporelings `−1`, `+1` forces focus (`05` §3.2, op `SET_TARGET_PRIORITY`). (b) Rimehold's Core is a **state flag, not a second actor** — `DAMAGE_TAKEN_MULT` ×1.6 at `05` §4 step 6, no targeting change. (c) Dicelord phase 2 becomes **`1–4: boss buff · 5–6: both buff`** — the hero-favourable outcome is removed. (d) **Enrage is `05` §3.1's form, authoritative over `17` §1's prose**: one built-in `SYS_ENRAGE` on every boss, `PERIODIC {interval 1.0, startDelay 70.0}` → `STAT_MULT ATK ×1.08`, multiplicative, uncapped, `BATTLE` scope, **tick slot 3**, bosses only.
2. **Sporequeen's Rot drain × the 70 s enrage: keep `SYS_ENRAGE` universal and unmodified; the drain is the effective timer.** `17` §11 requires the enrage "implemented once, applied to all bosses", and a per-boss opt-out used by one of eight is exactly the bespoke boss code `18` exists to prevent. At par power the fight ends in 35–60 s, so neither timer is reached — the interaction is only reachable by a build that is already failing. The verification `17` §8 asks for becomes a **harness assertion in M2-16a** (Sporequeen's median duration stays in the 35–60 s band), not an engine change. 🔴 The "**O13**" label on this item was a **mis-reference** — O13 in `16` Part B is *curse chapter gating*, an M3 item — corrected here.
3. **`CP_GLASS_HEART` stays ×2, with the ×1.6 downgrade pre-agreed as the automatic response** to a >12 pp clear-rate swing (`05` §9 guardrail 2) — no second conversation needed, it is a data edit. ⚠️ The trigger **cannot fire in M2**: measuring a per-perk swing needs the 98-perk catalogue (M3-07). Recorded for **M2-16b**.
4. **M2-16 is split.** **M2-16a** (in M2) ships the harness, CLI, nightly CI job (X-08) and **five** live guardrails (1, 3, 4, 5, 6) driven by `calibration_builds.json`'s archetype **statlines** — `par_power.json` and `calibration_builds.json` are already fully populated. **M2-16b** ⛔ defers guardrail 2 to **M3-07b**. ⚠️ The archetypes' `frozenPerks`/`pets` reference ids that do not exist until M3-07/M4-07; M2-16a reads only `stats` and must not stub the rest.
5. **M2-17 is reinterpreted as a committed-baseline determinism test** — 10 000 seeded random build permutations through `EffectResolver`, hashed with `CanonicalStateWriter`, checked against a committed reference table. There is one resolver in one assembly and no client build until M7, so there is no second implementation to compare against; real two-runtime parity stays with **M5-12**. ⚠️ The baseline is necessarily **self-generated** — S5 does not apply and must not be implied. It proves *stability*, not correctness; correctness comes from M2-02..M2-06's unit tests.

**Assumptions recorded after the interactive window** (full reasoning in the kickoff record)

- **`data/…` in the docs is `game-data/…`, and combat data lives under `content/`, not `tuning/`.** `game-data/content/combat_caps.json`, `content/enemies/`, `content/bosses/`, each with a schema in `game-data/schema/` citing its doc section. Honours M0's committed baseline ruling (`closedBy: M2-07`); `tuning/` stays at exactly 16 files. ⚠️ The baseline's reason mis-attributes that ruling to `16` A7, which never mentions combat caps — conclusion stands, citation is wrong, M2-07 corrects it.
- **`LogHash` reuses `CanonicalStateWriter`** (M0-07), the repo's single FNV-1a serialiser (`14` §16.6). M2-15 extends its allowlist if `CombatEvent` needs an encoding; it never forks it.
- **No M2 type is named `*Snapshot` and no `SnapshotFieldOrder.json` entry is touched.** `05` §1's `heroSnapshot`/`enemySnapshot` are combat *inputs*, not persisted state; naming them `*Snapshot` would drag a battle input into the persistence contract.
- **The run layer is declared, not wired.** The 12 run/board ops, 6 run-side triggers, the `RUN` target and the `RUN`/`STAGE`/`PERMANENT` scopes belong to a run controller (**M3**) over a `Run` aggregate (**M1-05**) — `18` §2.5 already says so. M2 declares and validates all of them, implements every combat-side trigger against the tick loop, and unit-tests the run-side ones at the resolver against a **read-only run-state view interface** with an in-Core test double. The exit criterion is met literally; M3 inherits wiring, not design.
- **`18` §2.5's combat-context exception is emit-only in M2.** Scramble appends `RunEffectQueued` to the log and nothing more; draining it is M3's, discarding it in a duel is M2-14's. No sixth field on `SimulationResult` — `05` §7 fixes its five.
- **The `< 5 ms` budget is a `Core.Tests` assertion** over the median of N worst-case (1800-tick) fights, at a documented multiple of 5 ms to survive CI jitter, with the raw median printed. No benchmark project.
- 🔴 **Cross-milestone: M1-06's new architecture rule must allow the battle simulator's `DeterministicRng` construction site.** M1 kickoff §5 makes the constructor unreachable outside `Core/Rng/` and `RunRngScope`, but battle draws are a different regime with **no persisted position** (a revived battle restarts at draw 0) and `SeedDerivation.BattleSeed`'s own committed doc comment prescribes `new DeterministicRng(battleSeed, RngStreams.Combat)`. Handed to the M1 conductor before M1-06 is dispatched.

## M3 — Board, dice & the run loop

**Goal:** a complete run — roll, move, resolve, fight, draft, die or win — playable through `InMemoryGame`.
**Exit:** full Chapter-1 **and Chapter-2** runs complete headless through commands only; board generator satisfies C1–C7 across seeds.

**Kickoff decisions** — ✅ resolved 2026-08-15 (record: `.claude/.milestone-runs/M3/kickoff.md`)
1. **Treasure-tile drops — ✅ RULED: never drop gear.** `03` §7a.3 stands as written — Crowns/Stones/Dust only, per its weighted payout profile table. `08` §6's "25 % gear chance" row is a stale doc erratum (pre-dates §7a.3's formula-authored table and has no supporting mechanic of its own); flagged for a doc fix, not applied here. *(Carried from the M0 review.)*
2. **Chapter-1/2 tile vocabulary — ✅ RULED: widened to match `03` §2.1's own Chapter-1/Stage-1 reference distribution exactly.** All 14 `TILE_*` kinds are live from Chapter 1 — the tracker's original "weighted 0, deferred to M11" plan for `TILE_EVENT`/`TILE_MINIGAME`/`TILE_CURSE`/`TILE_DICE_FORGE` contradicted `03` §2.1's worked example (all four have nonzero weight there) and silently omitted `TILE_EMPTY`/`TILE_PORTAL` entirely. Nothing is deferred to M11 for the tile vocabulary itself; M11 adds later-chapter *data rows* (weights, curse gating beyond Ch1-2), never new resolver types. Consequences below.
3. **O13 — curse chapter gating — ✅ RULED, folding back into `16_DECISION_LOG.md`:** `CUR_SLIPPERY`, `CUR_MARKED`, `CUR_DIZZY`, `CUR_FRACTURED` from Chapter 1; the remaining 8 from Chapter 3 onward; `CUR_HUNTED` specifically from Chapter 5 — `19_CONTENT_TABLES.md`'s own suggestion, accepted as-is. **Only the first four are in M3's actual scope** (Ch1–2); the rest are M11-02 data rows, no new code.
4. **`TILE_MINIGAME` split out of M3-03 (new task M3-03c).** `03` §6 fully specifies all 4 minigames (rules + chapter-scaled reward tables + a locked server-authority ruling distinguishing server-rolled from client-asserted-with-legality-check), but it's 4 distinct outcome/legality tables — a qualitatively bigger lift than an ordinary tile resolver. M3 builds only the headless Core resolution (`InMemoryGame`, no UI); the 4 interactive screens are M7's, same as every other M3 tile.
5. **`TILE_DICE_FORGE`'s content gap closed inside M3-04, not deferred.** The tile promises a player choice of which face to upgrade and how, but `18` §7.9's only worked `MODIFY_DIE_FACE` example is one hardcoded outcome — no authored upgrade-options table exists. M3-04 now includes authoring that table (which faces, which tiers, any cost) alongside the dice system it already owns, closing the gap rather than shipping a single-outcome stub.
6. **`TILE_CURSE`/`TILE_EVENT` fold into M3-03/M3-14 as ordinary resolvers** — both are fully content-authored (12 curses in `19` Part E, 30/30 event cards in `19` Part A), so no new design work, just wider resolver/data scope.

**Net effect on scope:** despite pulling in 6 more tile kinds, only `TILE_EVENT` (generic card resolver, real but small), 4 curses' worth of `TILE_CURSE` data, and the dice-forge upgrade table are genuinely new surface — `TILE_EMPTY`/`TILE_PORTAL` are trivial/already-covered by M3-02, and `TILE_MINIGAME` is isolated into its own task rather than inflating M3-03.

| M3-07R | **Perk rework + the wire that makes a perk mean anything.** Replace `06` §2–3's six categories and 82-row pool with NINE categories — five elements (`LIGHTNING`, `COLD`, `FIRE`, `POISON`, `BLEED`) and four generic (`DEFENSE`, `OFFENSE`, `CRIT`, `SUSTAIN`) — each entered through ONE base perk that gates the rest, and make a drafted perk actually reach the tick loop | 06 §2–3, 05 §5, 18 §8 | ✅ — 🔴 **The defect underneath was that owning a perk did nothing at all.** `PERKS` was 18 §8 step 1's source 10 of 10 and had never been wired: the draft wrote `{perkId: tier}` into the run, `HeroBuild` composed only the three gear sources, and no code anywhere read a tier's `effects`. A player could take Sharp Edge and fight exactly as hard as one who skipped it, with every test green. `Rules.Effects.PerkEffectSource` + `Content.Perks.PerkEffects` (a full-vocabulary reader; `PerkCatalogue` deliberately stops at `TierCount`) close it, and `EffectSourceCatalogue`'s pending row and its `SubjectSetFloorTests` entry are discharged (expiry floor 7→6). **Catalogue**: 67 rows / 237 authored effects, zero per-perk code. The gate is `requires`, enforced by narrowing `PerkDraftEngine`'s draftable pool — which also enforced NEITHER `requires` nor `excludes` before this, on a catalogue where both were empty on every row. Depth is capped at one step by test. **Statuses 12→13**: `CHILL` appended (LAST — a row position is `LogHash`'s wire ordinal); `POISON` moved from `TARGET_MAX_HP_PCT_PER_SECOND` to `APPLIER_ATK_PCT_PER_SECOND` and lost its ceiling of 3 (the five elements ARE their stacking rules); `BLEED` moved from `NONE`+refresh to `ADDITIVE` maxStacks 5 so a perk has stacks to consume. `EffectStackSet` already read a null ceiling as uncapped — no engine change needed. `enemies.json`'s Ch2 Bog Rot retuned to the ATK basis (0.015 target-Max-HP → 0.20 caster ATK) because the tick arithmetic reads the CATALOGUE's basis, not the on-hit row's. ⚠️ **Conditions read the effect's HOLDER, not its target** (`ConditionEvaluator`), so "is this ENEMY chilled" and "scale by the target's bleed stacks" are sentences the DSL cannot say; the affected perks apply to the enemies they strike instead. The shipped `PK_RUPTURE_T3_BONUS` scaled on `STATUS_STACKS BLEED` and had this bug — it is gone with the row. **Deleted**: M3-07's unauthored-perks register and its test (the catalogue was replaced, not completed) — see the struck-through M3-07b row in M11. `calibration_builds.json`'s five archetypes rewritten against the new catalogue, base-before-gated asserted; the forward-reference exemption narrowed 22 ids → 3 (the pet perks, which no system authors). `06` §2–3 rewritten from the data; `05` §5 carries the three status changes. Perk-category colours repainted 6→9 — mutual separation falls to 20° and the rationale says so rather than claiming otherwise. New tests at the outermost seams: a drafted perk changes the stat block and the tier matters (`DraftedPerkEffectTests`); an owned ailment perk applies AND ticks its ailment in a real fight and changes the replay hash (`DraftedPerkFightTests`); a run owning nothing is offered only un-gated perks, and owning a base opens exactly what requires it (`PerkDraftEngineTests`). Unauthorised-holes 278→**291**, all canonical DSL null-tokens. **Opening draft**: `Handlers.StartRun` now opens a perk draft on the run it just built, so a run's first decision is a perk on the same three-option draft the rest of the run uses. Spelled `MarkDraftPending(TileKind.Empty, stage 1)` — `CurrentDraft` asks the kind only whether it is Elite or Boss, so `Empty` is the honest reading (no battle caused it) and draws the ordinary stage-1 band; writing `Enemy` would key the same band off a battle the run never fought. No new state, no schema bump, and no client change: `BoardPresenter` already routes on `DraftPending` however the draft was opened. Because a run owning nothing satisfies no prerequisite, the opening draft offers three of the nine BASES — it is the choice of element, which is what makes the gate a decision rather than a delay. ⚠️ `GameRules.Apply`'s draft gate refuses `ABANDON_RUN` while a draft is open, so a player must answer the opening draft before leaving the run; pre-existing behaviour of every draft, surfaced here for the first time and recorded in `06` §1. Three shared fixtures now skip it (`Application.Tests.UseCases.Worlds.InARun`, `TwoPlayersOneEndedRun`, the two host command cases). Core 5998 · Application 1164 · Client 509 · Contract 160 · Architecture 173/173 — whole solution green |
## M4 — Luck, gear & the hero (Core)

**Goal:** the between-run loop that a run actually feeds — loot, forge, inventory and the hero — as pure Core rules. 🔒 `LuckService` lands **here**, before any grant path exists.
**Exit:** a simulated player runs, banks gear, merges and enhances it, levels the hero and carries the loadout into the next run entirely in memory; every grant path routes through `LuckService` (architecture test).

**Kickoff decisions** — ✅ resolved 2026-08-16, **`--non-interactive`: every answer is `[auto-accepted]`** (record: `.claude/.milestone-runs/M4/kickoff.md`)

1. **Rarity-floor renormalisation — ✅ RULED `[auto-accepted]`: proportional, one rule for every source class.** A floor zeroes the weight of every rarity below it, then the surviving entries are rescaled to sum to 1 **preserving their relative ratios**. Grounded in `24` §4.0a rule 2, the only renormalisation method the document commits to anywhere ("the multiplier applies to the SS weight *before* normalisation; the other shares shrink **proportionally**") — floors are the same operation with the sign reversed, and proportional rescale is the unique method that keeps the §1.1 *Disclosure* odds page truthful. **No per-class variation**: §4.0a rule 3 is stated once over "a source with a stated floor" and nothing in `24` differentiates. Counter behaviour is unchanged — a floored draw advances and resets counters normally (rule 3's own sentence). Discharged in data as a new `rarityFloor` block in `game-data/tuning/luck.json` + `luck.schema.json` (X-03: the 📐 must live in `tuning/`), read by `LuckService` from exactly one place.
2. **`DRAFT` source class — ✅ RULED `[auto-accepted]`: exactly five rules, and the tracker's own "five vs six" framing was wrong.** Neither doc says six. `24` §4.7 lists **2 standing + 3 additions = 5**; `06` §4 lists **8 bullets** while claiming "all five rules above". The five `DRAFT`-class rules resolved by `LuckService` are: **Legendary pity (draft #15)**, **Sustain anti-brick**, **F1 quality floor**, **F2 Codex bias**, **F3 upgrade famine**. `06` §4's first three bullets — **no duplicate options**, **category diversity**, **owned-upgrade bias (30 %)** — are *draft composition rules*, not luck protections: they stay in `Rules/Perks/` and never touch a counter. Doc deliverable: `06` §4's "All five rules above" → "the last five rules above". Data deliverable: `luck.json#/draft` is **missing `sustainAntiBrick` entirely** — add it (§4.7 says it "stands"); leave `ownedUpgradeBias` where it already sits (inside `upgradeFamine`, which is exactly how §4.7's F3 states it).
3. **`30` §11.4 / `Events → Model` — ✅ RULED `[auto-accepted]`: permitted, narrowly, with a new architecture rule carrying the restriction.** An event **may** name a `Model/` type **only** when that type is an immutable, fully-serialisable value record with no `internal` mutators — snapshot-shaped, like `08` §7's flat `GearInstance`. It may **never** name an aggregate *root* (`Player`, `Run`) or any `Model/` type that carries `internal` mutators. `GearGranted(int, GearInstance, SourceClass, bool)` is therefore written as `30` §7 sketches it. Deliverables, all in M4-03: (a) amend `30` §11.4 — the chain becomes `Testing → Handlers → Rules → Model → Content → Primitives` with `Commands` and `Events` as **peer leaves** (both may name `Content`/`Primitives`/`Rng`; `Commands` may **not** name `Model`; `Events` may, under the value-record restriction); (b) add the rule to `Core_internal_layering_holds`, S1-proven with two shapes plus a negative control; (c) amend `DomainEvent`'s own doc comment, which currently says an event may hold "not an aggregate" and would otherwise contradict the ruling in the same file. *(Closes M1 carry-forward 8.)*
4. **Inventory capacity — ✅ RULED `[auto-accepted]`: 320 is authoritative; `08` §5's "up to 400" is a stale erratum.** `10` §4's ladder is explicitly marked **🔒 Reconciliation (ruled in `16` A7)** — the later, ruled text, and A7 is this tracker's own authority chain. Base **120** + a hard cap of **10 purchases × +20 = 320**. Supporting evidence that 400 is a transcription slip: `10` §2 prices the flat Soul-Shard alternative at **400 Soul Shards**, and `currencies.json` carries `INVENTORY_EXPANSION_FLAT: 400` — the same number one line away from a capacity. Deliverables: `08` §5 doc fix; `forge.json#/inventory` `maxCapacity` 400 → **320** and the `maxCapacityReachableFromLadder` key + its CONFLICT `_doc` deleted. *(Also halves M1 carry-forward 12's worst case: 320 slots, not 400.)*

⚠️ **Kickoff decision 4 above (inventory capacity 320) was SUPERSEDED on 2026-08-17** by the M4 retro's product-owner ruling, logged as `16` **A9 / D36**: capacity is a **flat 1000** (`baseCapacity == maxCapacity == 1000`) and `EXPAND_INVENTORY` was **explicitly refused**, so no command can move the ceiling. `10` §4's Crown ladder and `10` §2's flat 400-Soul-Shard alternative stay authored, priced and **unspent — deferred, not deleted**. `08` §5 already carries the errata and `InventoryTuning.Read` enforces the deferral in two arms (the ladder's reach must stay **strictly above** the ceiling, and the ceiling must equal the base). 🔒 The 320 in M4-05's task description below is therefore **stale prose kept for provenance, not a live number** — the measured consequence of the real cap is in `.claude/retros/M4.md` §4: 117.6–167.4 ms per 720 commands at 1000 slots against `30` §6's 200 ms budget, where 320 slots measured 101.8 ms. The thin headroom is the finding; the next cap increase is a `30` §4.1 narrower-slice or copy-on-write decision, not a data edit.

**Readiness review (Phase 2) — 10 further items, all resolved `[auto-accepted]`.** Full reasoning in the kickoff record; the load-bearing ones:

- **M4-01 is split.** `LuckService` itself (primitives, registry, counters, routing rule, property tests) is one run; **wiring the grant paths that already exist** is another — the new **M4-01b**. M3-06 left **8 draft composition rules stubbed as documented no-ops** and M3-03c left `MG_CHEST_PICK` drawing **uniformly** with `24` §4.9's pity flagged as a `LuckService` gap. Both are inert seams waiting on this milestone; neither belongs inside M4-01.
- **S22 sweep — what M4 does *not* deliver.** `M4-04b` (Reforge, Retune, **Focus ×2.5**, Set-Token redemption) is scheduled in **M9**, not here, even though `24` §5–§6 specifies it and `luck.json` already carries all of its tuning. M4-01 exposes the identity-roll seam Focus will bias and **must not implement Focus**. Chest/egg/crate/wheel grant paths (`M4-02`, `M4-07`, `M4-08`, `M4-09`) are likewise M9's. So the exit criterion's "every grant path routes through `LuckService`" is asserted over the grant paths that **exist at the end of M4** — `DROP_RUN`, `ENHANCE`, `DRAFT`, `MINIGAME` — and the architecture rule is written to bite the day a fifth appears.
- **Enhance success-rate interpolation — ✅ RULED: linear between the authored band endpoints, evenly spaced across each band's five levels.** `08` §4.2 writes `85% → 65%` and `50% → 25%`; the arrow **is** the doc's ramp notation, so both bands are fully determined by numbers already authored: `+6..+10 = 85/80/75/70/65` and `+11..+15 = 50/43.75/37.5/31.25/25`. Authored into `forge.json`, discharging the deliberate `null` at `forge.schema.json:92`. **No number is invented** — every value is a function of two published endpoints. Without this M4's exit criterion ("merges and **enhances** it") is unreachable above +5.
- **Type homes, pre-assigned so two lanes cannot both declare them (S23's shape, applied to types):** `SourceClass` (10 ids) and `Rarity` (C/B/A/S/SS) are **`Primitives/`, authored by M4-01**. `GearSlot` (`WEAPON`/`HELMET`/`ARMOR`/`BOOTS`/`RING`/`AMULET`) and `GearFamily` (24) are **`Primitives/`, authored by M4-03**, which retypes the nine gear-slot command payloads in the same commit (**closes M1 carry-forward 15**). `GearInstance` is **`Model/Gear/`** — a component of the `Player` aggregate, not a root.
- 🔴 **`SnapshotSchema.SchemaVersion` — R5's reservation scheme was NOT IMPLEMENTABLE and was corrected mid-run (kickoff record §A9).** `SnapshotFieldOrderPinTests` iterates `Enumerable.Range(1, SchemaVersion)` and demands a pin section for **every intervening version**, so a task jumping to a reserved-but-higher number would have to invent other tasks' serialisation layouts. **A bumping task can only ever take `current + 1`; numbers settle at MERGE ORDER, not in advance.** S23's real value — making each lane *aware* a shared counter exists — stands and worked: every agent reported its number unprompted, so no collision went silent. The pre-assignment half was mechanism invented without checking the rule that governs the counter (S21, committed by the conductor). Settled: **9 M4-13 · 10 M4-01b · 11 M4-05** (corrected by message while it was still running) · M4-04 and M4-10 take `base + 1` with **no reserved integer**.
- **Inventory overflow — ✅ RULED:** at capacity a grant is **held, never destroyed** (`24`'s founding rule). Core models an explicit overflow holding list on `Player`; held items are not equippable, mergeable or salvageable until the player expands or clears space. The inbox delivery surface `28` A describes is **M5-08's**, not M4's.
- **Presets — ✅ RULED:** M4-10 stores only what exists in Core at M4 (the gear loadout), with the record shaped so M4-06/07/08 (talents/pets/mount) and M12 (PvP set) **add fields, not structure**. The slot count stays **O11's** (M9 kickoff): M4-10 reads the free allowance from `ads.json#/freePresets` and answers `NOT_ENTITLED` beyond it without Plus (`12` §2.1, `14` §16.2) — authored data, not an invented bound.
- **Legend Level — ✅ RULED:** the **formula** `LegendXpForLevel(L) = 120 × L^1.05` is authoritative; `07` §1.1's cumulative column is illustrative and contradicts its own prose (`~3.04M` in the table vs `~3.07M` two lines later). Compute from the formula; transcribe neither rounded total. Doc fix flagged.
- **Profanity filter — ✅ RULED:** `07` §1 gives only "12 chars, profanity-filtered, default *Wanderer*". `27` §1 is the design set's **only** authored profanity specification (EN + DE, at creation **and on every edit**) — apply that same standard to the hero name rather than running two. The word **list** is content, not a mechanic: it ships as `game-data/content/profanity/{en,de}.json` with a conservative, documented seed set and a self-expiring register entry naming **M17** as the owner of the curated list. D20 still binds — nothing machine-translated.
- **M4-13 vs O29 — ✅ RULED:** build the **mechanism**, freeze **no vocabulary**. A generic additive counter map on `Player`, driven by a small closed event→counter projection table in code (`28` D: Feats are "evaluated server-side from the event stream"), covering only counters derivable from events that already exist. No feat definitions, no thresholds, no `feats.json`. **O29 stays open to M16**, guarded by a `GapRegister` entry.
- **New task M4-15** carries the milestone's exit criterion itself, which no existing row owned.

Fixed with literal composed figures (`ATK == 112.0` = `36 + 1000 × 0.1 × 3.8 × 0.2`) and a five-ring fixture that **overshoots** the cap, so equality at the ceiling kills both *no cap* and *cap per contribution*. | M4-16c | 🔴 **`STAT_ADD_PCT` on a zero-based stat is inert, and the SHIPPED PERK CATALOGUE is full of it.** Found by M4-16 while deciding its own affix ops; **re-verified independently by the conductor against `game-data/` before being written here (S9/S21)**, not taken from the report. `StatAggregation` implements `Final = (Base + Σ FlatAdd) × (1 + Σ PctAdd)`, and `content/combat_caps.json` gives `DMG_PCT`, `DR_PCT`, `LIFESTEAL`, `BLOCK`, `PEN` and `THORNS` a base of **0.0**. Measured in the shipped content: **15 `STAT_ADD_PCT DMG_PCT` clauses** (`PK_BRUTALITY`, `PK_EXECUTIONER`, `PK_GIANT_SLAYER` …), **3 `STAT_ADD_PCT DR_PCT`** (`PK_LAST_STAND`, whose Tier III reads *"+70 % damage reduction"*), and **1 `STAT_ADD_PCT CRIT`** (base 0.05, so ~1/20th of intent). **Eighteen perk clauses currently do nothing at all.** `18`’s own worked examples do the same (Ossify: `STAT_ADD_PCT DR_PCT 0.30`), so this is a genuine design-vs-implementation contradiction that **predates M4** and is exactly why M4-16 mapped those affixes `STAT_ADD_FLAT`. 🔒 **Either reading is one data edit away in both directions** — the op is authored per row — so the cost of the ruling is the ruling, not the refactor. ⚠️ It must be settled **before M6 tunes anything**, or the simulator calibrates against perks that do not fire | 05 §1.1, 18, 06 | 🔄 **WAS BLOCKED — the 2026-08-18 ruling landed and did not close it; the 2026-08-20 follow-up at the end of this cell does.** 🔒 **Product owner ruled 2026-08-18: give `DMG%`/`DR%` a non-zero base and retune, rather than re-authoring the 18 clauses as `STAT_ADD_FLAT`.** The ruling's *intent* is unambiguous and is recorded as decided: **the perks stay as written and the stat model yields.** 🔴 **But the value cannot be 1.00, and it was measured before anything was edited (S9/S21) — the illustrative `0.00 → 1.00` shown when the ruling was put was WRONG in one case and catastrophic in the other.** `AttackPipeline.cs:82` consumes the first as `ATK × mult × (1.0 + DMG_PCT)` and `AttackPipeline.cs:331` consumes the second as `damage × (1.0 - DR_PCT)`. So a base of `1.00` makes every actor deal **double** damage and take **none at all** — `DR% = 1.00` is total invulnerability and no fight in the game could ever end. 🔴 **And no smaller non-zero base rescues it either, because `0.00` is these stats' IDENTITY value, not an unset one.** They are additive *modifier* stats consumed inside a `(1 ± x)` term, so any non-zero base is a permanent global buff or nerf, and a small one (say `0.05`) reproduces exactly the ~1/20th-of-intent defect the single `CRIT` clause already has — a *"+15% Damage"* perk would deliver **0.75 percentage points** — extending the bug to 18 more clauses instead of fixing it. 🔒 **What the ruling therefore needs is one more decision, and it is a smaller one:** the ruling is implementable if these stats become MULTIPLIER stats rather than additive ones — base `1.00`, with `AttackPipeline` consuming them bare (`× DMG_PCT`, `× DR_PCT`) instead of inside `(1 ± x)`. That is clean for `DMG%`: `1.00 × 1.15 = 1.15` is exactly what *"+15% Damage"* means. ⚠️ It is **not** clean for `DR%`, because that stat is a *reduction*: a positive `STAT_ADD_PCT 0.30` would raise damage taken by 30%, so either the 3 clauses re-sign or `DR%` inverts — and note `DAMAGE_TAKEN_MULT` already exists as a separate op with 6 uses, which is the shape `DR%` would be converging on. ⛔ **Deliberately NOT implemented here.** It changes `05` §1.1's aggregation contract and the damage formula every M6 calibration target is derived against, which is far outside the M4+M7 completion run's scope; and one arm of it (`DR%`'s sign) is still an open product question. **OWNER: the M7 milestone review**, which now carries a decided direction plus one narrow follow-up rather than an open question. 🔒 Unchanged: it must be settled before M6 tunes anything ✅ **RULED 2026-08-20 (D46) — the narrow follow-up is answered: `DMG%`/`DR%` become MULTIPLIER stats** (base `1.00`, consumed bare), **and the 3 `STAT_ADD_PCT DR_PCT` clauses RE-SIGN** as damage-taken multipliers below `1.00`. One uniform rule for both stats, rather than converging `DR%` onto `DAMAGE_TAKEN_MULT` — which stays a separate op with its 6 uses. Owner: **M4-16d**, no longer the M7 milestone review. 🔒 Unchanged and now dated: it lands **before M6 starts** |

**Exit criteria — 🔒 re-verified by the M4 milestone review on `review/M4`, 2026-08-17. ✅ ALL SIX CLAUSES NOW MET — but read the second column before reading that as M4's achievement.**

🔴 **The honest headline: M4 shipped MET IN PART, and three of the four open clauses were closed by M7, not by M4.** M7 was kicked off on top of `milestone/M4` and pulled M4's two blocking defects in as first-class tasks (M7-00a/X-10, M7-00b/X-09) plus the grant-and-equip wiring (M7-00d). This review branched off `main` rather than off `milestone/M4` precisely so the verdicts below describe the repository as it actually stands; the *as-delivered-at-`milestone/M4`* verdicts are preserved in the third column so the milestone is not credited with work it did not do.

| Clause | Verdict now (`review/M4`) | How it is known · and what it was at `milestone/M4` |
|---|---|---|
| a simulated player **runs** | ✅ met | `MetaLoopTests.A_simulated_player_plays_a_whole_run_through_commands_alone` and `A_run_crosses_both_stage_boundaries_and_ends_in_a_victory` — through `Apply` only. **Was ✅ at M4**, though only stage 1 was reachable until M7-00a |
| **banks gear** | ✅ met | `MetaLoopTests.A_run_banks_gear_into_the_players_own_stock`. `ConfirmBattleResult` and `EndRun` now call `GearGeneration` → `Inventory.Place` → `GearGranted`. **Was ⛔ at M4** — no production caller existed anywhere in `src/`. **Closed by M7-00d, not by M4** |
| **merges and enhances it** | ✅ met | **Closed by this review.** The forge half ran on a *seeded* stock on a *different board* from the banking case, so the clause's *"…and enhances **it**"* was never one chain. `A_run_banks_gear_into_the_players_own_stock` now drives `ENHANCE` on the item the run actually banked and asserts the level or the failure counter moved. **Was ⚠️ at M4** |
| **levels the hero** | ✅ met | `MetaLoopTests.A_run_that_wins_levels_the_hero_past_the_first_Legend_rung`, asserted through all three effects (lifetime XP over the rung, the level, the Talent Point, the `legend_level_up` Energy row). **Was ⚠️ at M4**: no reachable run crossed the 120-XP rung, best 93 XP of 372 swept — because no run could finish. **Closed by M7-00a/00e/00f**, which made a victory reachable |
| **carries the loadout into the next run** | ✅ met | `A_second_run_starts_after_the_first_ends_and_carries_the_players_loadout` **and** `The_loadout_carried_into_a_run_is_the_one_EQUIP_filled` — the second is what makes the byte comparison discriminate, since an empty loadout has one canonical encoding. **Was ⛔ at M4** (X-09 + X-10). **Closed by M7-00b and M7-00d** |
| every grant path routes through `LuckService` | ✅ met | `LuckRoutingRuleTests` — four live classes (`DROP_RUN`, `ENHANCE`, `DRAFT`, `MINIGAME`) in **both directions**, stated over the façade member because enum tokens fold to `ldc.i4` (S18); a fifth path fails the build. **Was ✅ at M4** and is the milestone's strongest artefact |

⚠️ **What "met" does NOT mean here, stated so nobody reads more into the row than it carries.** The loadout is **carried and inert**: `Run.StartingLoadout` is written at `START_RUN` and read by nothing but `ToSnapshot`/`Rehydrate`, and `GearStatDerivation`, `SetBonusResolver`, `InventorySorting` and `UnlockGate` have **zero production callers**. Gear therefore has no mechanical effect on combat anywhere in the repo. The exit criterion as written does not ask for one — it asks that the loadout be carried — so this is not a failed clause, but the *milestone goal's* "loop that a run actually feeds" is only half-delivered. See **M4-16** below and M7-06b, which is the same hole seen from the client.

🔴 **Two Core defects M4-15 found, both older than M4, both verified in source by the conductor before being written here (S9/S21). ✅ BOTH FIXED IN M7 — X-10 by M7-00a, X-09 by M7-00b. Kept in full because they are the evidence behind the exit table above and behind steering rule S25.**

| # | Defect | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **X-09** | **`Apply` gives a player exactly one run, ever.** `GameRules.cs`'s run-present guard refuses **every** `CommandKind.Run` command — `START_RUN` included — with `RUN_ALREADY_ENDED` once `Phase == Ended`, and nothing in `Apply` clears `WorldSlice.Run`. M3-15's `OpensRun` flag exempts `START_RUN` from the run-**less** guard only; there is no equivalent for the run-**present** one | No second run. Blocks M4's carry-the-loadout clause and **M7's entire exit criterion** |
| **X-10** | **`MovementEngine.Advance` soft-locks at every stage boundary.** The stage-end clamp returns the run *unmoved with the roll unspent* whenever the next node changes stage. Its comment — *"`current` is already that node, since every edge but this one has already been taken"* — holds on the roll that first **reaches** the boundary, but on the next roll the run is already standing there, so the first loop iteration clamps again. `03` §1.1 authors the clamp as a **one-time** stop and nothing consumes it | A command-driven run **can never leave stage 1**. The boss, a victory, and `END_RUN` via victory are all unreachable. **Repairing it is one line and breaks only M4-15's 5 cases — 5 863 other Core tests stay green**, which is precisely why nothing caught it |

🔴 **Consequence for M3, recorded rather than left standing.** M3's exit criterion reads *"full Chapter-1 **and Chapter-2** runs complete headless through commands only"*. **Under X-10 that claim is overstated** — no command-driven run leaves stage 1. M3's own tests exercised the movement engine and the resolvers directly rather than driving a whole run through `Apply`, which is exactly the gap S24 was written about after M3's review found two tile kinds a run could never leave. M4-15 is the first test in the repo that drives the **whole** loop through commands, and it found this on its first attempt.

🔴 **Consequence for M7 — its exit criterion is unreachable until X-09 and X-10 are fixed.** M7 requires *"full Chapter-1 and Chapter-2 runs on an Android device: roll → move → fight → draft → results, **gear banked and equipped between runs**"*. That needs X-10 (to finish a run), X-09 (to start a second), the `DROP_RUN` grant wiring currently scheduled as **M4-02** in M9, and an `EQUIP` handler still `Deferred`. **All four are carried into the M7 kickoff as first-class scope**, not left as notes.

**Overarching tasks advanced:** **X-02** 123 → **129** rules — 🔒 *re-measured by the review at `d34607f`, not quoted: `Invoke-UnitTests.ps1 -Group architecture` reports 129/129.* **X-03** unchanged in mechanism; unauthorised holes 276 → **264**, mechanically pinned by `RealDataNegativeCaseTests.The_shipped_data_set_still_carries_exactly_its_264_unauthorised_holes` (M4-03's row says 265 — that was its mid-run measurement; later tasks closed one more). **X-01/X-04/X-05/X-06/X-08** unchanged. *(The whole M4 snapshot row was re-verified the same way: Core **5872** · Application **855** · Architecture **129** · Contract **24**, all green at the milestone head. Every figure is exact.)*

### 🔒 M4 milestone review — 2026-08-17, branch `review/M4`

**Range reviewed:** `aa2c9a0..d34607f` — 56 commits, 243 files, +41,306/−493. *(`milestone/M4`'s branch pointer sits one commit further, at `6d0e560` "Kick off M7", which edits only this tracker.)* Branched off `main` rather than off the milestone head, deliberately: M7 sits on top of M4 and had already fixed X-09/X-10, so fixes cut from the milestone head would have fought M7 at merge and re-derived work already done.

**Dimensions run:** tests · code · architecture · cross-task consistency. **UI and mobile-UX skipped** — M4 touched no `.tscn`, `.tres`, theme resource or Godot file; it is pure `Core` + `game-data` + tests, verified from the diff.

🔒 **No integration/E2E tier was reintroduced.** No new `.csproj` under `tests/`, `test-suites.json` untouched, and every hit for `docker` / `Testcontainers` / `WebApplicationFactory` / `Integration` in the added test lines is a comment *reaffirming* the ban.

**Suites on `review/M4`:** unit **8165** (Core 6032 · Application 1060 · Client 348 · Contract 148 · Asset suites 577) · architecture **164**. Zero failures, build 0 warnings / 0 errors. *(Baseline at branch point: 8147 / 159.)*

| # | Fix applied | Dimension |
|---|---|---|

Found independently by three of the four dimensions; proved red-then-green before fixing | cross-task |

New rule requires every concrete `DomainEvent` to be projected or on a closed exemption list with a named owner; the handled set is read from the projection's own IL, never transcribed. **No counter ids invented** — that would pre-empt O29 | cross-task |

A third instance (`09 §53`) was found outside the brief. `30` §11.4's directory tree and `ARCHITECTURE.md` were five namespaces short and pointed `Economy/` at merge/enhance, which live in `Rules/Forge/` | architecture |

**Two findings rejected on inspection, both verified against the repo (S21 — the repo wins over an agent's claim):** a proposed `+1` on the chest-pick guarantee would have moved a live fairness guarantee by a draw (both keys already implement `24` §4.3 D1's *"On the 6th, force"* correctly — the defect was the name); and precomputing the Legend ladder would be **strictly worse**, because `LegendCurveTuning.Read` is called *inside* `Reconcile`, i.e. per command.

⚠️ **Process defect committed by the conductor, recorded because it nearly shipped one.** Two fix agents were given one checkout with only a file-territory split — **S10 forbids exactly this**. One agent's S1 mutation probes leaked into the other's production files twice, including `ChestPickGuarantee.GuaranteeFires => true`, which forces a gold chest on **every** pick. The sibling caught it, reverted both, and stopped rather than report a green result it could not trust. Both lines were then re-verified by the conductor directly, the whole production diff was read for other leaked probes, and a BOM/line-ending audit over all changed files found one stray UTF-8 BOM. **A green suite does not detect a reverted-then-reapplied probe; only reading the diff does.** → steering **S26**.

**Carried forward — findings too large to fix in a review pass, each with a named owner.** None is silently dropped; the first three are the load-bearing ones.

| Finding | Why not fixed here | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **Gear is inert. Nothing composes an equipped item into a stat.** `GearStatDerivation`, `SetBonusResolver`, `InventorySorting`, `UnlockGate` and `InventoryComparison` all have **zero production callers**, and `Run.StartingLoadout` is read by nothing. ## M5 — Server backbone, persistence & inbox

**Goal:** the ASP.NET Core host, persistence, auth and the wire contract — the game becomes a service, and the client's ports move from the in-process host to HTTPS. 🔒 The **inbox is built here** (step 4), not with the other live-service features.
**Exit:** a device plays a full run over HTTPS against the compose stack; reconnect chaos test passes; cross-platform determinism CI live.

**Kickoff decisions** — ✅ resolved 2026-08-26, **`--non-interactive`: every answer is `[auto-accepted]`** (record: `.claude/.milestone-runs/M5/kickoff.md`, incl. 12 readiness rulings R1–R12 the rows below reference)
1. **O5 — ✅ FCM directly (HTTP v1), no wrapper; APNs deferred with D34** (iOS post-launch). The port stays provider-neutral but is **not declared in M5** — no verifiable implementation, no consumer until M16; M5-08's COMPENSATION push is a named absence with owner M16.
2. **O33 — ✅ newest wins, confirmed — enforced structurally, no eviction machinery at v1.** Run-command sequencing already makes the last submitter the single writer; token families are per-device; any token eviction would be defeated by `16.5`'s own device-secret fallback.
3. **O34 — ✅ names NOT unique** (identity = player id); `07` §1 stands (12 chars, `27` §1 filter, default "Wanderer" — M5-06 wires `HeroNameRule`); rename surface not in M5, no cooldown invented; sanction = forced reset to default, recorded as sanction kind `NAME_RESET` (M5-14).
4. **O6 — ✅ confirmed deliberately open** (deploy-time; D22 makes the swap one adapter). Migrations target vanilla Postgres.
5. **`runSeed` — ✅ `02` §2 wins; `14` §16.6's Input row yields** (`14` §8.1 itself agrees with `02`). The wire `stateHash`/client mirror/parity hashes cover the **client-visible projection: `PlayerSnapshot` + `RunSnapshot` minus `RunSeed`**, hashed by the one `CanonicalStateWriter` with its own field-order pin. `RunSnapshot` persists `RunSeed` unchanged — no `SchemaVersion` bump. Doc edits land with M5-03; M5-12 asserts parity over the projection. Closes M1 carry-forward 10.

## M6 — Power model & economy simulator ∥

**Goal:** the product owner's dial (`29`) and the tool that grades the game against it (`21`). Starts the moment `InMemoryGame` + the meta systems it models exist (end of M9) — it runs against `Core` alone and waits on no client or server work.
**Exit:** `EconomySim assert` runs in CI on every `Data` change; first full 180-day × 14-profile run produced (expected to fail — that is its job); frontier curve derived.

⛔ **KICKOFF REFUSED AT PREFLIGHT — 2026-08-20 (`/kickoff-milestone M6 --non-interactive`).** Not deferred for capacity: three independent blockers, each verified against the repo rather than read off this tracker (S21).

1. 🔴 **The milestone's own start condition is false.** This section's goal says M6 *"starts the moment `InMemoryGame` + the meta systems it models exist (end of M9)"*. `21`'s `DayLoop`/`RunModel` model income from pets, mounts, talents, containers, codex, wallet/shop/dailies, dungeons, events, guilds and ads/Plus. **Measured: `Core` has no `Pets`, `Mounts`, `Talents`, `Codex` or `Containers` directory at any accessibility.** Nine M4 rows for those systems sit ⬜ in M9's table, and M10/M13/M14/M15 own the rest. An agent building `RunModel` now would **invent nine systems**, and every number it produced would be fiction.
2. 🔴 **D45 and D46 change the model M6 would calibrate against, and both are still ⬜.** D46's own consequences column says it *"must land before M6 tunes anything, or the simulator calibrates against perks that do not fire"*; D45 moves every fight outcome. `PowerCalculator` computes `EffectiveHP × DPS` over exactly the `AttackPipeline` arithmetic D46 rewrites. **M4-16d and M7-06g are hard prerequisites of this milestone**, not neighbours of it.
3. 🔴 **M6-09 cannot be scoped yet.** It wires *"A1–A16 + inherited E1–E23"*, but E6–E10 are M10-04's, E11–E15 are M13-07's and E16–E19 are M14-08's — all ⬜ — and this tracker's own **E19 ordering gate** says dungeons + events + guilds + Reforge/Retune sinks are tuned **together at M18, never individually**.

🔒 **What preflight DID establish, and it is worth reading before the next attempt: four rows are misreported as ⬜ below.** `PowerCalculator.cs` (225 lines, geometric form + `additive_legacy` switch + reference opponent + cap ordering) already exists, written by **M2-16a** at commit `b189b12`, with its two holes named in code rather than defaulted (`AbsentPetDpsShare`, `AbsentRegenPerSecond`) and `kPower` left `null` so `Compute` throws and callers use `PowerIndex`. `par_power.json` (94 numbers, 0 nulls), `sim_profiles.json` (346 numbers, 4 nulls), `calibration_builds.json` (109 numbers, rewritten by M3-07R) and `expected_progression.json` (8 checkpoint days, all three tolerance ladders, 14 profiles, `_status: transcribed`) are all authored. ⚠️ **A future kickoff must audit these four rows rather than dispatch them, or an agent will rebuild `PowerCalculator` beside the one the guardrails already grade** (S12).

🔒 **Two of the three kickoff decisions below are already answered in the data** — decision 3's ladders and Wide-band assignments are authored in `expected_progression.json`, and decision 2 is an owner act that file's own `_doc` already records (*"this file is the product owner's, not engineering's"*). **Only O1 is genuinely open**, and M6-10 is what derives it. So the decisions were never this milestone's blocker; the three faults above are.

**Kickoff decisions**
1. **O1** — intent for the frontier-bonus/catch-up curve (the simulator's first derivation target; −60 %/+50 % are placeholders).
2. Product owner reviews and commits `expected_progression.json` **before** the first run (changes to it are its own commits — R12).
3. Confirm tolerance ladders and which profiles use the Wide band.

## M7 — Application seam & the local playable client

**Goal:** the game on a phone — chapters 1–2, with placeholder art where M8 hasn't delivered yet. The client is composed against the **ports**, and this milestone provides the ports, the use-case layer and an in-process host behind them; M5 later swaps that host for HTTPS without touching a call site.
**Exit:** full Chapter-1 and Chapter-2 runs on an Android device: roll → move → fight → draft → results, gear banked and equipped between runs; CI builds the APK through the custom export path.

🔒 **M5-01/M5-02/M7-09 depend only on M3** and may run alongside M4; nothing in this milestone waits on the rest of M5.

**Kickoff decisions** — ✅ resolved 2026-08-16, **`--non-interactive`: every answer is `[auto-accepted]`** (record: `.claude/.milestone-runs/M7/kickoff.md`)

1. **O15 — ✅ carried, already closed 2026-08-14: hand-rolled composition root + presenter pattern**, per `23` §9. No DI container; **no task may introduce one**. O15b's scheduled re-review (2026-11-16) is untouched.
2. **Placeholder-asset policy — ✅ carried, answered by M8-10** (merged to `main`). Placeholders are a **build artifact under `artifacts/`, never committed**; every real asset later overwrites its placeholder **by id**. ⚠️ M8-10's caveat carries: its *"asserts the QA gate passes"* was unachievable as written (17 thresholds `null` per S6, 5 Part F items human-classified), so **no M7 task may treat placeholder QA as a gate it can turn green**. 🔴 **CORRECTED AT THE M7-03 MERGE — this decision, as written, overstated what M8-10 delivers, and the error is the conductor's**: it was taken from M8-10's tracker row (*"driven through M8-06's full pipeline into the §D2 atlases"*) without checking the artefact. M7-03 found four structural reasons the *"placeholder-atlas load"* clause cannot be met as written: `artifacts/placeholders/` has **never been generated on this machine**; the generator emits **loose per-asset PNGs and a rects-only JSON and never rasterises an atlas page**; the three `ui_loading_*` slots are **permanently skipped** (`deliverySize` *and* `pivot` both null, as are all 86 §E17 rows); and policy forbids placeholder output entering the Godot checkout at all. M7-03 built the load **stage** for real against M8-10's *actual* emitted format and made absence a named non-fatal outcome — **it did not fabricate an atlas**, which is the right call. Every later screen task inherits this: **there is no atlas to load.** M8-02/M8-03 remain ⛔, so **placeholders are the only visual source for this whole milestone** — exactly as M7's goal anticipates.
3. **Local host persistence — ✅ CONFIRMED: one local profile, disposable pre-launch data, no migration code.** `Rehydrate` hard-fails **loudly** on an unknown `SchemaVersion`. Reinforced by evidence: `SchemaVersion` moved **9 → 13 during M4 alone**, so five silent upgrades would have been five silent corruptions.
4. **O8 — ✅ carried, closed at M8: procedural in-engine VFX.** The decision log routes it *"carry into the M7 and M9 kickoffs"*: **VFX is Godot particle/shader work, never a sprite sheet**, and no task may add a VFX asset row. All 32 E19 sheets were already cut (975 → 943).
5. 🔒 **Device play — RULED, splitting the exit criterion (S15).** Verified directly on this machine: Godot **4.7.1-stable mono** + **mono** export templates (incl. `android_source.zip`), **JDK 17**, and a full **Android SDK** at `G:\tools\android-sdk` are all present — so the M0-05a export path is locally reproducible. **In scope:** the APK builds headlessly through the custom export template and is asserted to **contain managed assemblies** (non-negotiable — a missing `.sln` makes the export exit 0 with a valid ~80 MB APK containing **zero .NET**, M0-05a finding 5); full Chapter-1/2 runs through the in-process host; the same runs through the Godot client on a **desktop export**. **⛔ Blocked:** a human playing two chapters on a **physical handset** (no agent can attach to or tap a device — a product-owner acceptance step), and **CI on a runner** (X-07: still no git remote). Stated at kickoff rather than discovered at the exit gate.
6. 🔒 **M4's two blocking defects are pulled into M7 as first-class tasks (M7-00a/b/d).** M7's exit criterion is unreachable without them no matter how good the client is: **X-09** (`Apply` gives a player exactly one run, ever) and **X-10** (movement soft-locks at every stage boundary, so no command-driven run leaves stage 1). Two gaps of the same class join them: **nothing banks a drop** (`Inventory.Place`/`GearGeneration`/`GearGranted` all exist, unconnected — the `DROP_RUN` half comes forward from M4-02), and **`EQUIP` is still `Deferred`**, so *"equipped between runs"* is unreachable.

**Readiness review (first kickoff, 2026-08-16) — 9 items, all `[auto-accepted]`.** The load-bearing ones: **client tasks never run in a worktree and never two at once** (the `.godot/` import cache is checkout-bound), so the eight Godot-touching rows **serialise in the main checkout** while worktree'd non-client work runs alongside — forcing parallelism there would corrupt the cache, not save time. **Presenters are plain C# with no engine reference** (`23` §9, `14` §5), which is what makes the client half unit-testable at all. `M5-01` lands **X-06** for the first time and ships the fake **and** the shared contract suite in the same change (**S7**). Out of scope, checked against every ⬜ row (**S22**): `M5-15`/`M7-02` stay in M5; `M9-01` keeps the Forge/inventory screens and auto-salvage UI; `M6-04` keeps `ExpectedPower(L)`, so **M7-04's par-power warning is not in scope**; the FTUE stays M9's.
